## 2026-11-20T13:39:00+00:00 - refine-draft
- **Status**: Success
- **Files**: [[topics/emotion-and-dualism]] (primary), [[topics/purpose-and-alignment]], [[apex/minds-without-words]]
- **Task**: P3 core-affect gloss reversal (todo L40-49): three pages said core affect theory holds "we are never affectively neutral"; the source holds that core affect is always present and may be neutral, and speaks of human experience. Author order also swapped on two pages.
- **Source ledger**: Russell & Barrett (1999), *JPSP* 76(5), 805-819. Abstract [raw-abs, PubMed efetch 10353204, tags stripped + unescaped, nonsense control 0]: "Core affect refers to consciously accessible elemental processes of pleasure and activation, has many causes, and is always present. Its structure involves two bipolar dimensions." (`is always present` count 1; `never affectively neutral` count 0.) Two bipolar dimensions give the neutral midpoint the paraphrase relies on. p. 806 ("a person is always in some state of core affect, even if neutral"): still NOT grep-verified this pass. The lab-hosted PDF URL now returns a "Page not found" page and the lab site is behind a WAF; scispace/typeset return 403 or empty; OpenAlex reports the work closed with no repository full text. So no page-806 sentence is quoted on any page edited here; the 2026-11-17 by-eye reading stands as recorded. Crossref (DOI 10.1037/0022-3514.76.5.805): authors James A. Russell, Lisa Feldman Barrett; 1999; *Journal of Personality and Social Psychology*; vol. 76, issue 5, pp. 805-819.
- **emotion-and-dualism, Core Affect section**: "Lisa Feldman Barrett and James Russell introduced "core affect"" became "James Russell and Lisa Feldman Barrett introduced "core affect""; "Core affect is always present; we are never affectively neutral." became "Core affect is always present in us, though it may be neutral."
- **emotion-and-dualism, Challenges (argument repair)**: "Core affect theory suggests we are never affectively neutral. If true, consciousness and valence may be inseparable. But meditative states of pure awareness might provide counterexamples." became "Core affect theory holds that human experience always carries some affective state, possibly neutral, so not every moment need feel good or bad. Whether consciousness without valence is possible in other kinds of minds is a question the Map leaves open; meditative states of pure awareness might test even the human claim." With the corrected premise, what follows is that in humans some affective state is constant, not that experience is always felt as good or bad. The inseparability conclusion is withdrawn and scoped. The open-question sentence is copied verbatim from `concepts/valence` L37. No claim that valence-free consciousness exists.
- **purpose-and-alignment, The Special Weight of Valence**: "humans are never affectively neutral. A truly aligned system would consider this continuous valenced character" became "humans are always in some affective state, possibly neutral. A truly aligned system would consider this continuous affective character". The continuity point the alignment argument uses survives the correction. The page names no author and carries no 1999 reference entry, so none was added.
- **minds-without-words, Emotional Consciousness**: "Barrett and Russell's [[emotion-and-dualism|core affect]] framework suggests this is not intermittent: consciousness carries valence at all times, blending pleasure-displeasure with arousal-deactivation. We are never affectively neutral." became "Russell and Barrett's [[emotion-and-dualism|core affect]] framework holds that in human experience some affective state, blending pleasure-displeasure with activation-deactivation, is always present, though it may be neutral." Scope decision: the apex never argues from this sentence to animals or infants. Its non-human valence claims rest on the Panksepp/Birch/LeDoux paragraph ("realistic possibility, contested"), and its moral-stakes paragraph rests on the valence criterion, not on "all consciousness is valenced". So scoping the sentence to human experience is enough, and no new sentence was added. "arousal-deactivation" corrected to the dimension's poles "activation-deactivation" at zero cost.
- **Reference entries**: emotion-and-dualism L223 and minds-without-words L181 already match Crossref (Russell, J. A., & Barrett, L. F.; 1999; JPSP 76(5), 805-819; DOI). Unchanged.
- **Not touched (other tasks own them)**: emotion-and-dualism L79 asymbolia sentence (blocked P3 ~L4534), the 12 uncited References and the year-less body mentions (P3 ~L4625, which also owns adding "1999" to the core-affect body mention); purpose-and-alignment L41/L91/L145/L151 (P3s ~L3926, ~L4010); minds-without-words L111 asymbolia sentence (P3 ~L4534).
- **Report only**: `concepts/valence` L41 "Barrett's constructionism ... treating valence and arousal as basic features of consciousness". This is substantially supported by Barrett & Bliss-Moreau (2009), *Adv. Exp. Soc. Psychol.* 41 (PMC2884406, text-mined): core affect is "a basic, universal, and psychologically irreducible property of the mind", and valence and arousal "describe something psychologically primitive". The source says "mind", not "consciousness": it treats background core affect as sometimes "unconscious", and the page cites no Barrett work at that sentence. A minor drift, not a reversal. Also still carrying the old gloss: `research/emotional-consciousness-valence-2026-01-19` L182 (the seed: "Core affect is always present—we are never affectively neutral") and the archived `archive/topics/emotional-consciousness` L97/L169 and `archive/concepts/emotional-consciousness` L101.
- **Engagement modes**: none; no named-opponent reply was edited.
- **Length** (analyze_length): emotion-and-dualism 3437 to 3465 (+28; hard 4000); purpose-and-alignment 2633 to 2637 (+4; hard 4000); minds-without-words 4149 to 4149 (0; hard 5000).
- **ai_system**: claude-opus-5-5 appended to purpose-and-alignment only; it was already present on the other two.

- **Status**: Success
- **Topic**: Iconic and discursive perceptual format and the richness question
- **Output**: [[research/iconic-and-discursive-perceptual-format-2026-11-20]]
- **Sources consulted**: 21 cited. Read in full or in the relevant parts: Block (2023) chs. 5 and 13 (the book is open access at Oxford Academic; printed pages), Quilty-Dunn (2020a, 2020b) via ORA author manuscripts, Beck's NDPR review and Firestone & Phillips's *Science* review. Read via the OpenAlex text index: Block's 2025 précis and the opening of his replies. Abstracts only: Gross & Flombaum 2017, Gross 2018, Green 2020/2023, Green & Quilty-Dunn 2021, Clarke 2022, Masciari 2021, Ji et al. 2024, Hill 2024, Block's BBS e265, Quilty-Dunn, Porot & Mandelbaum 2023, Tian et al. 2025. Leads, unread: Fodor 2007, Kosslyn, Carey 2009, Burge 2010, Lau et al. 2022, Quilty-Dunn 2025. PhilArchive returned a Cloudflare challenge and Wiley a 403.
- **Key finding**: format gives a third option at the level of representation, and both camps can grant it. Block (p. 444) splits overflow into whether perceptual capacity exceeds cognitive capacity ("clearly yes") and "Is the excess capacity conscious?". He allows that a global-workspace theorist "can accept a kind of overflow" of perception over cognition (p. 445). The phenomenal question stays open, but the cost of each answer shifts: a format border plus VWM-only consciousness implies "visual representations are never conscious" (Quilty-Dunn 2020a). Gemini's review inverts Quilty-Dunn (2020a), which argues that iconic memory *is* iconic.
- **Verdict**: `fold` (topics 370/370, concepts 370/370, voids 124/125). The primary host is `concepts/phenomenal-overflow`, with a new H3 `{#format}` after "Rival Readings" of ≤ 340 words (model text 333; 2,793 → ~3,126). Optional secondaries: `topics/visual-indexes-and-demonstrative-reference` (object files, ≤ 150), `concepts/cognitive-penetration` (≤ 100) and `concepts/perception` (≤ 70). No edit to `voids/plenitude-void`, which is over its gate, or to `topics/dualist-perception`, which has 9 words of room. No task minted: a fold mints nothing, so the driver must hand-mint the refine-draft.

## 2026-11-20T00:32:40+00:00 - research-topic
- **Status**: Success
- **Topic**: Confucian and Xunzian mourning ritual and the proper duration of grief
- **Output**: [[research/confucian-mourning-ritual-and-grief-2026-11-20]]
- **Sources consulted**: 25 cited. Primary texts read in Chinese (Wikisource: *Analects* 3, 11, 17, 19; *Xunzi* 19; *Liji* "Tangong" and "Sannian wen"; *Mencius* 3A, 7A; *Xiaojing* 18; *Mozi* "Jiezang"; Zhu Xi's *Lunyu jizhu*) and in public-domain English (Legge 1872, 1875, 1879, 1885a, 1885b; Dubs 1928; Internet Archive scans, every quotation grep-verified); SEP "Xunzi", "Confucius", "Mencius" and Olberding's 2017 *Aeon* essay in full; Chan et al. 2026, Liu 2006, Choo 2019, Foust 2021, Li 2014, Fraser 2013, Ing 2012, Olberding 2004/2023, Elder 2020, Tan 2026 at abstract level. ctext.org declined automated access and was not used. Leads: Puqun Li 2017, Olberding 2008/2011, Virág 2022, Hutton 2014
- **Key finding**: the texts set grief's term by the rite, graded to the relationship, and end mourning in the 25th month while "The sorrow and pain were not yet ended", on the ground of duties to the living. On the Map's reading this relocates Marušić's puzzle (from feeling to rite) rather than dissolving it, and leans on the very needs-of-the-living reason Marušić says explains without justifying. The firm contribution is to self-knowledge: Zai Wo's ease, Zilu's reluctance, Boyu's excess and Confucius's own "Is it excessive?" over Yan Hui (Zhu Xi: grief at its height does not know itself) are all treated as poor guides to where grief's proper course ends. - **Sources consulted**: 29 cited (11 read in full text: Lenferink et al. 2019, Comtesse et al. 2023, Testoni et al. 2020, Isuru et al. 2021, Blandin & Pepin 2017, Givens et al. 2011, Ponce et al. 2026, Manevich & Haber 2026 in part, Paun et al. 2022, Lucas et al. 2022, Partridge et al. 2025; Boss's University of Minnesota site; the rest abstracts or metadata), plus 8 leads (Tierney, Cholbi, Kauffman, Dupuis, Boss's books)
- **Key finding**: Boss's frozen-grief and most-stressful claims are not borne out by comparative studies (no difference in psychopathology between relatives of the missing and the deceased; homicide-bereaved higher in a Dutch sample), though hope and time to death confirmation predict worse grief. For the Map: the bereavement void's lag needs a fact, and when someone disappears there is none, so the expectation may be warranted hope and the state resists its own name. In dementia the fact splits: carers grieve before the death and rarely recognise it as grief. Type Two bears on the Map's identity verdict only as a practical test of meaning, not as evidence either way
- **Verdict**: `fold`. Host A `topics/phenomenology-of-attention-to-absence` "Social absence" +209 (~3,428 → ~3,637 after booked folds); Host B `topics/consciousness-and-neurodegenerative-disease` end of "The Identity Crisis" +289 (3,632 → 3,921); zero-word pipes in `voids/bereavement-void` (stays 2,989) and `voids/beloved-void` (stays 2,978). ## 2026-11-19T14:38:51+00:00 - refine-draft
- **Status**: Success
- **File**: [[apex/open-question-ai-consciousness]] (primary); [[concepts/temporal-consciousness]]; [[apex/time-consciousness-growing-block]]
- **Review file**: [[reviews/outer-review-2026-11-19-gemini-4-neon]] (§A "Clinical Misrepresentation of Temporal Disintegration in Schizophrenia", weakness 5)
- **Original score**: n/a (`scripts/curate.py` absent); one targeted clause per file
- **Verdict**: the full text of Fuchs (2007) does not support "while within-moment experience remains intact". It places the disturbance in the retention–protention synthesis that, on Fuchs's Husserlian model, is part of each present moment ("consciousness is not momentary"), and it says single elements of experience become "alienated or opaque" and self-awareness is disturbed. What it does support is that patients stay conscious, and become more explicitly aware than usual. All three copies of the clause were reworded so that they keep only what the paper supports.
- **Source ledger**: Fuchs, T. (2007), "The Temporal Structure of Intentionality and Its Disturbance in Schizophrenia", *Psychopathology* 40(4), 229–235, doi 10.1159/000101365. Abstract from PubMed efetch 17396049. Full text from the author's copy at klinikum.uni-heidelberg.de/fileadmin/zpm/psychatrie/fuchs/Intentionality.pdf (7 pp., header "Psychopathology 2007;40:229–235", 4,437 words after pdftotext and whitespace collapse). Karger PDF returned 403; Unpaywall and Semantic Scholar list it as closed. Raw greps of the full text: "intact" 0, "preserved" 0, "spared" 0, "unimpair" 0, "unaffect" 0, "remain" 0; "within" 1 (p. 231, "lost within the sentence", which is unrelated); "self-awareness" 2; positive control "intentional arc" 19. Verbatim, each count 1:
  - p. 229 (abstract): "disturbances of self-awareness" among the basic symptoms "caused by a weakening and dissolution of the intentional arc"; "A failure of the continuous intertwining of succeeding moments, and especially of the protentional function".
  - p. 230 (thesis): "leaving single elements of perception, action and thought processes unconnected. Instead of carrying the intentional arc, they come to explicit awareness as alienated or opaque phenomena."
  - p. 231: "Thus consciousness is not momentary but spreads out by its double-faced attention of retention and protention"; the arc carries "what could be called an ‘implicit self-awareness’".
  - p. 233: "Patients have to compensate for this by an awareness of each single movement" (this supports "patients remain conscious").
  - p. 234 (conclusion): "In schizophrenia the protentional function may be weakened in such a way that the constant intertwining of succeeding conscious moments fails"; "the normal transparency of consciousness turns into opacity".
  - p. 235, the passage closest to the Map's clause: "patients are able to live from one moment to the next without experiencing a disruption of time". It describes a compensatory strategy (minimising change), not intact within-moment experience.
- **Changes**:
  - `apex/open-question-ai-consciousness` L111, first sentence only. OLD "Temporal disintegration in schizophrenia (Fuchs, 2007) disrupts binding between successive moments while within-moment experience remains intact;" NEW "Schizophrenic temporal disintegration (Fuchs, 2007) weakens protention and the intertwining of moments while patients remain conscious;" (16 → 16 words). The driver's suggested NEW was +3 by `split()`, which would break net ≤ 0, so the term was shortened instead. The paragraph's inference ("while consciousness continues") needs only that consciousness continues, so it still holds. The flow-states clause, the Pinto sentence and the split-brain "One calibration" sentence were not touched.
  - `concepts/temporal-consciousness` L154. OLD "**Temporal disintegration in schizophrenia** disrupts binding between moments while leaving within-moment experience intact (Fuchs, 2007)." NEW "**Temporal disintegration in schizophrenia** weakens protention, and with it the intertwining of successive moments, while patients remain conscious (Fuchs, 2007)." Naming protention ties the case to the page's own L128 definition and to its micro/meso scale split (L152), since the disturbance reaches inside the specious present.
  - `apex/time-consciousness-growing-block` L119. OLD "disrupts binding between successive moments while leaving within-moment experience intact—actions feel disconnected from consequences." NEW "disrupts binding between successive moments—actions feel disconnected from consequences—while patients remain conscious." The first half, which the paper supports, is kept. The next sentence's "Temporal unity is therefore contingent" now rests on a stated premise.
- **Not fixed, passed on**: the growing-block clause "actions feel disconnected from consequences" is not in Fuchs (2007). The nearest passage is p. 233: "the temporal disintegration of action units results in a loss of everyday habits or a ‘disautomation’". The sentence does not cite Fuchs: it links to `concepts/temporal-consciousness`, which does not mention actions. The phrase is copied from `topics/temporal-consciousness-structure-and-agency` L196, where it is uncited. It falls outside this task's clause, so it was left alone.
- **Sibling check**: `grep -rl within-moment` over topics/, concepts/, apex/ and voids/ found these three files before the edit and 0 after. Other schizophrenia lines that pair with "intact" or "preserved" are agency and ownership claims sourced to Gallagher, Nichols and Stich, not this gloss. `topics/clinical-phenomenology-and-altered-experience` L101 ("disrupts the *micro-level*") makes no intactness claim.

- **Length** (`analyze_length`, whitespace `split()`): apex/open-question-ai-consciousness 6,017 → 6,017 (0); apex prose (body minus frontmatter, yt-embed, Source Articles, Further Reading, References; reproduces 4,828 on `082c5b9842`) 4,982 → 4,982 (0). concepts/temporal-consciousness 3,061 → 3,066 (+5, against a ≤ +10 budget). apex/time-consciousness-growing-block 4,641 → 4,639 (−2).

Verified strings: "There are at least two reasons why one might find the regress argument, thus understood, less than persuasive"; "it ultimately seems to be the fact that he endorses a form of idealism about time that provides Husserl's principal motivation for adopting an intentionalist, rather than an extensionalist view"; "it is not clear whether Husserl ultimately provides good grounds for favouring the analysis he ends up over a rival, extensionalist, approach"; necessary extension claim "shared by extensionalist views"; conclusion: the flow remarks express "ideas that are ultimately better accommodated by ... an extensionalist model". - **Status**: Success

- **Critical issues addressed**: 3
  - The Laukkonen et al. (2025) paraphrase said "the inferences that most reduce long-term uncertainty win entry to the world model" and called the competition "the selector". OLD: `makes inferential competition the selector — "Bayesian binding," where the inferences that most reduce long-term uncertainty win entry to the world model, no valence term denominating the contest;` NEW: `makes inferential competition decide what enters the world model: "Only the inferences that coherently reduce long-term uncertainty win, evincing a selection for consciousness that we call Bayesian binding." Valence appears only as one contextual signal neuromodulators may carry into precision control.` The paper says "coherently", not "most". Its selection is a "selection for consciousness". Stapp and Chalmers–McQueen support only the weaker L143 version ("disagree on which physical systems can host consciousness").
- **Kent / Q-shape check (the Gemini lead)**: the Q-shape answer is CONFIRMED, but Kent's direction is INVERTED.
  - In the Chalmers–McQueen arXiv full text (2105.02314v1), "Q-shape" appears 151 times. The scalar-Φ equal-measure problem is their own argument: "Φ is not a genuine physical correlate of consciousness … It is merely a physical correlate of a scalar degree of consciousness, where the same degree can be present in many different conscious states". They illustrate it with the blue/green case.

Kent therefore does not contest Chalmers–McQueen's model as a support for Four, and Chalmers–McQueen was not counted as contested on Kent's ground.

It says "Kent (2021) shows that a *scalar* Φ leaves no gradient between distinct experiences of equal measure (seeing red versus green, each Φ=k), so their superposition would persist macroscopically". That is Chalmers–McQueen's argument (blue/green), and Kent's paper rebuts it. Its "Q-shape" escape route is Chalmers–McQueen's own. This is a refine-draft candidate. The Gemini review's "refutes symmetry premise against single-measure collapse" is accurate; its "refuting Chalmers and McQueen's symmetry argument for Q-shape collapse" (weakness 3) is accurate too, but it overstates the result as damaging the model.

Mallozzi, Vaidya and Wallner (2023) summarise his position as the view that "we ought to withhold judgment concerning what we conceive or imagine or infer about modal reality, when they are outside the range of reliability of the specified capacity or method." (2) "Yanofsky (2017) frames as an intrinsic limitation of physics" misused an essay that says "the limitations of physics are not inherent to the objects studied" and does not discuss consciousness; moved to the Kantian layer, re-dated 2020 (PDF created 2020-03-01, cites the author's own 2017 contest essay). (3) Witten "2014" was the broadcaster's upload date (2014-07-21), not the interview date; year removed; his "very unlikely" on modified brain physics added. (4) Bremermann's figure (c²/h, 1.35 × 10⁴⁷ per gram per second in the source, 1.36 × 10⁵⁰ per kilogram with present constants) is in Bremermann 1967, which calls 1962 a preliminary announcement; 1967 added; "caps computation" scoped to a closed system's channel capacity. (5) "absolute ceilings... No brain, no quantum computer, no future technology can exceed them" made conditional; Lloyd 2000's 5.4258 × 10⁵⁰ operations per second for 1 kg shows the figure depends on what is counted. (6) Landauer's principle is a minimum cost per bit erased, not a ceiling on processing; Bérut 2012 "matching the theoretical minimum" became the abstract's result (mean heat approaches the bound as erasure is slowed). (7) Bekenstein bound reworded to entropy of a bounded system of given energy; reference added. (8) Tenet section brought to P-V3: "may itself be evidence that consciousness has aspects outside the nomic domain" removed; no tenet is supported by the void; the non-physical-mind escape is a marked speculation bounded by the interface. (9) No Many Worlds paragraph conflated Everett branches (same constants) with a landscape of law-sets; rewritten.
- **Medium issues addressed**: 5. Lead and description now mark the step from limits on physical systems to unreachable thoughts as the Map's conjecture; Wheeler's registering observer distinguished from the Map's consciousness reading; Chomsky's sentence marked as about biological endowment; AI sentence brought to P-AC1; Wolfram gloss replaced by his own words ("can lead to utterly and incoherently different basic descriptions of the universe").

- **Engagement modes (editor's note)**: Carroll: boundary-marking; the Map's reply is declared a commitment and not a rebuttal on his ground.
- **UNOBTAINED**: Bremermann 1962, Bekenstein 1981 and Wheeler 1990 texts; the Witten interview year and the video itself (read through Jogalekar's 2016 transcript); Carroll's published pagination (open-access draft read); Yanofsky's FQXi venue.

- **File**: [[voids/binding-void]]

- **Lens**: source fidelity. 28 quotations and attributed claims read at source where obtainable (Bayne & Chalmers 2003, James 1890 ch. VI, James 1909 Lecture V, McGinn 1989, Deroy et al. 2016 and the Stanford Encyclopedia entry in full text; Tononi 2008, Revonsuo 1999 and de Haan et al. 2020 by abstract). Dainton 2000, Tye 2003 and Dennett 1991 were not obtained.
- **Critical issues addressed**: 9. (1) "strongly supports", credited to the Stanford Encyclopedia, is not in the entry; removed. (2) The Encyclopedia's three doubts about introspective evidence for unity were used as support for a hidden mechanism, with an invented continuation ("that prevent access to the mechanism"); now quoted in their own sense and said to cut against the page's starting point. (3) IIT was credited with identifying "necessary conditions ... without revealing sufficient conditions"; Tononi 2008 claims an identity, and the objection is now marked as the Map's. (4) "James concluded that the compounding of consciousness is ... fundamentally unintelligible" and "James showed in 1890": his target was the mind-stuff theory, he held the whole thought to be a single new state, and in 1909 he said his chapter had done "little more than quote" earlier critics and gave up "the logic"; paragraph rewritten and the Evidence item regraded. (5) "every proposed analysis" scoped to the two rival analyses surveyed, in the body, the Evidence list and the tenet section; the Encyclopedia's "sufficiently ampliative" concern and its unexamined relation-first analyses (Masrour, Watzl, Roelofs) now stated. (6) Feature binding separated from phenomenal unity (Revonsuo 1999 quoted; binding by synchrony stated as a contested hypothesis, Shadlen & Movshon 1999). (7) Bayne & Chalmers split-brain quotation restored to lower case with its condition ("the possibility they hold open"), and Bayne's later move to a "switch model" added. (8) Split-brain "extraordinarily opaque" and McGinn "finds one of its strongest applications here" regraded: opacity to date per de Haan et al. 2020, and McGinn's 1989 paper is naturalistic and does not discuss unity. (9) Lead, description and classification brought to the body's grade: the classification is stated as the Map's, argued and not established, with the functional and no-determinate-unity objections named and left unanswered in-framework.
- **Medium issues addressed**: 5. Unverified Dainton wording removed; Tye described as the Encyclopedia describes him, and the unverified "frames his own account against" Dainton replaced; Deroy quotation given its own subject, with the authors' access-based suggestion; "standardly objected to" became "has been objected to (Hill, 2018, ...)"; references: Chalmers 1996 and Bayne 2010 removed as orphans, Dennett 1991, Hill 2018, Revonsuo 1999 and Shadlen & Movshon 1999 added.
- **Enhancements made**: 2 (the feature-binding and unity distinction; the circularity worry tied to the literature).
- **Reasoning mode**: engagement with Dennett: Mode Three, unchanged. Engagement with IIT: Mode Two shading into Three; the identity is asserted, the proponents' denial is stated. Engagement with the physicalist reading of the gap: Mode Three.
- **Tenet section**: regraded 2026-11-16 and holds. Two forced edits only (datum sentence rescoped; "standardly").
- **Cuts that funded the corrections, verbatim**: "Every other void on the Map can be encountered or avoided depending on what one thinks about. The binding void is present in every conscious moment—the precondition for having any experience at all, yet it disappears the instant one tries to examine it. Not because it hides, but because examination already presupposes it." (compressed to one sentence) | "It is the most fundamental feature of phenomenal experience, and any attempt to define it in terms of something else either fails or smuggles unity back in under a different name." | "Only Dainton holds that decomposition stops here;" (stated two sentences earlier) | "No amount of detail about the temporal pattern explains how it generates a seamless experiential field." | "the Capgras-prosopagnosia double dissociation shows that what seems like a single act of recognition is at minimum two distinct contributions—perceptual identification and affective familiarity—bound together invisibly" (shortened) | "The binding is not an object within consciousness; it is the condition that makes consciousness a unified space in which objects can appear." | "examining binding through introspection would be like trying to see one's own eyes without a mirror" | "The binding void occupies a precise position in the Map's void taxonomy." | "It asks how the already-present elements of a single experience cohere into one field." | "Binding concerns internal coherence; boundary-and-projection concerns external limits and their dynamic extensions." | "—how its elements achieve coherence" | "—its hidden reliability weights and its hidden common-cause gate" | "—it is the foundation on which the self-reference paradox stands" | "subsumption for an encompassing total experience, the one-experience view for a single encompassing content" and "Bayne, Chalmers and Tye press their analyses as improvements on primitivism" (Evidence item; both stated in the body) | "Locating without explaining is characteristic of void territory." | "Every waking moment presents a unified field." | "The thought begins to form, encounters its own presupposition, and dissolves." | ", suggesting the phenomenon occupies a region of conceptual space that ordinary categories do not reach" | "The barrier is architectural rather than a failure of effort: the binding mechanism is the substrate on which observation depends." (restated by the constitutive-barrier item) | "The void may be actively maintained by the very process it conceals." | nine Further Reading descriptions (voids, binding-problem, boundary-and-projection, emergence-void, three-kinds-of-void, causal-interface, self-opacity, mysterianism, fusion-void; the links stay). Strings grepped in v3: "three conditions that seem necessary for consciousness"; "the most basal or minimal form of awareness is a highly simplified (nearly contentless) world model knowing itself non-locally"; the global model, "however minimal, is necessary but not sufficient for conscious experience. It is only when this global posterior is reflected back through the underlying hierarchy that the conditions for consciousness are met"; "consciousness clearly precedes introspection or complex metacognition"; "this is achieved through recursion not metacognition"; "MPE occurs when: epistemic depth is maximally high, and the reality model is contentless"; "the most truly minimal versions of MPE occur during deep sleep"; deep slow-wave sleep "results in a minimal, or transiently absent, reality model" with low epistemic depth; cessations are "periods of total absence wherein no experience occurs" and "This Bayesian unbinding of the reality model includes MPE; the world model is not contentless but incoherent"; epistemic depth lets the hyper-model "opacify, introspect, interrogate, and therefore change the nature of contents in the reality model"; footnote 11, "Our position does not assert that satisfying these conditions guarantees phenomenal experience in the subjective sense". What this supports: reflexive self-knowing ("epistemic depth") is one of three conditions the paper holds necessary for any consciousness, so it is part of the minimal state and not something a later act adds; minimal phenomenal experience is modelled as that knowing at its maximum over a contentless model, so on the paper the contemplative floor is the minimum itself and not a floor lying above it; nothing is conscious beneath the loop. What it does NOT support: that the paper rejects the enrichment premise as the page states it. The paper separates its reflexivity from introspection and metacognition and says introspection changes the contents it interrogates, which is congenial to that premise. The disagreement therefore falls first on the existence premise (no fainter experience beneath the loop, and the minimum is reached and known), and only in part on the enrichment premise (the knowing in the minimal state is constitutive, not added). The outer review's claim that on this model "the enrichment premise fails" is stronger than the paper. - **Status**: Success
- **Files**: [[concepts/valence]] (primary), [[voids/valence-void]]
- **Source**: optimistic review 2026-11-17, priority item 2 and finding 3.3 ([[reviews/optimistic-2026-11-17-ethics-of-consciousness-wing]])
- **Why**: both pages stated flatly that every conscious experience is valenced; taken literally that settles the valence question the invertebrate page, the Vulcan passage on sentientism and P-MS1 all hold open. The source the concept page leans on does not say it either.
- **Source ledger**: Russell & Feldman Barrett (1999), *JPSP* 76(5), 805-819. Abstract [raw-abs, PubMed 10353204]: "Core affect refers to consciously accessible elemental processes of pleasure and activation, has many causes, and is always present." Body, p. 806 [raw, read by eye from the page image of the scanned lab-hosted PDF; no text layer, so not grep-verified]: "Core affective feelings vary in intensity, and a person is always in some state of core affect, even if neutral (Diener & Iran-Nejad, 1986; Diener, Sandvik, & Pavot, 1991). When mild, core affect can fade into the background of consciousness". The page's gloss "we are never affectively neutral" therefore reversed the source, which holds that core affect is always present and may be neutral, and speaks of "a person". Russell (2003), *Psychological Review* 110(1): abstract only [raw-abs, PubMed 12529060], which does not address neutrality; full text unobtained and not cited.
- **valence, What Valence Is, sentence 1**: "Every conscious experience carries some degree of valence." became "In human experience, as core affect theory describes it, some hedonic state is always present, though it may be neutral."
- **valence, same paragraph, source sentence**: "James Russell and Lisa Feldman Barrett's core affect theory holds that we are never affectively neutral—a background hedonic tone is always present, combining valence (pleasure-displeasure) with arousal (activation-deactivation)." became "James Russell and Lisa Feldman Barrett's core affect theory holds that "a person is always in some state of core affect, even if neutral" (1999, p. 806), a feeling combining valence (pleasure-displeasure) with arousal (activation-deactivation)."
- **valence, added sentence**: "Whether consciousness without valence is possible in other kinds of minds is a question the Map leaves open; the [[sentientism#^vulcans|philosophical Vulcan]] case poses it and the [[valence-void]] follows both answers." Anchor `^vulcans` confirmed on concepts/sentientism. No claim that valence-free consciousness exists.
- **valence-void, lead**: "Every conscious experience carries hedonic tone — pleasant, unpleasant, or nominally neutral —" became "Human experience as we know it carries hedonic tone — pleasant, unpleasant, or nominally neutral —". No core-affect attribution added, since the void page does not cite that literature. The other sentences of the lead, which the pending tenet-section task owns, are untouched, as is Relation to Site Perspective.
- **valence-void, The Circularity Problem (premise use)**: "since valence appears to be constitutive of experience." became "since valence appears, in our own case, to be constitutive of experience (whether it must be is held open below)." This was the one place either page argued from the universal, and it pre-empted the page's own two-horn section.
- **Premise-use sweep**: neither page steps from "all experience is valenced" to "consciousness suffices for moral status"; valence's moral-status section makes valence the necessary condition and keeps the invertebrate expectation as "a framework commitment, not a probability". Left as they stand: valence-void "Every moment of experience demonstrates the phenomenon" and "every probe carries hedonic tone" (about our own inquiry), the vedanā gloss (attributed to Buddhist analysis), "If true affective neutrality is unreachable" (conditional); valence's Panksepp sentence (attributed, mammals).
- **Untouched commitments**: valence as intrinsic phenomenal character, its causal relevance and its role as ground of moral significance are unchanged; neither Russell nor Barrett is enlisted for dualism.
- **Reported, not edited**: the reversed gloss or the flat universal also stands at topics/emotion-and-dualism L113 ("Core affect is always present; we are never affectively neutral", author order also reversed) and L159, topics/purpose-and-alignment L107 ("humans are never affectively neutral"), apex/minds-without-words L109 ("consciousness carries valence at all times", "Barrett and Russell").
- **Length** (analyze_length): valence 2425 to 2474 (+49; hard 3500); valence-void 2291 to 2306 (+15; hard 3000).

## 2026-11-17T08:14:52+00:00 - research-voids
- **Status**: Success
- **Topic**: The learning void: what a person can tell about their own learning while it is happening
- **Category**: Mixed (Unexplorable in a weak, time-bound sense; Occluded in the architectural sense). A narrow limit with a known remedy.
- **Output**: [[research/voids-learning-void-2026-11-17]]
- **Capacity**: `count_section_files('voids')` 123 of 125 at 08:03Z.
- **Key finding**: Judgements of learning made during practice follow ease of processing, and under spacing, interleaving and self-testing they have been shown to favour the worse method (Kornell and Bjork 2008: 78% did better with spacing and 78% rated massing as good or better); delayed self-testing gives accurate access, and the misjudgement gave way in Yan et al.'s sixth experiment.
- **Sources**: 4 read in full text (one from an OCR scan), 14 as PubMed abstracts, 1 metadata only. 71 strings script-checked. Soderstrom and Bjork (2015) full text not obtained after four routes.
- **Counter-evidence**: one aimed search found no failed replication; the note records the delayed-judgement meta-analysis (Rhodes and Tauber 2011), the material dependence of the interleaving benefit (Brunmair and Richter 2019) and the disputed disfluency meta-analysis as limits.
- **Tenet grade**: no tenet supported; ordinary cue-utilisation accounts of metamemory predict the limit, so it does not discriminate between dualism and physicalism.
- **Recommended action**: `article`, slug `learning-void`, title "The Learning Void". Fold fallback: a paragraph under 190 words in `concepts/anti-correlated-metacognitive-signal`.
- **Not done**: `evolution-state.yaml` not touched (the driver holds an uncommitted change to it and mints the follow-on task); `todo.md` not touched; nothing committed.

## 2026-11-16T11:01:35+00:00 - research-voids
- **Status**: Success
- **Topic**: The bereavement void (comprehending another person's death: the lag between believing and absorbing, and the fading of grief while the loss stays the same)
- **Category**: Mixed (Unexplorable in a weak, time-bound sense for the lag; Occluded on the adaptive reading for the lapse)
- **Output**: [[research/voids-bereavement-void-2026-11-16]]
- **Recommended action**: `article`, proposed slug `bereavement-void`
- **Key finding**: Sensed presence of the dead is common among the bereaved (reviews give 30–60% and 47–82%) and coexists with knowing the person has died, while Marušić (2018) and Moller (2007) argue that grief's fading is not answerable to the griever's reasons and leaves the loss unregistered; a physicalist account of attachment learning and hedonic adaptation predicts all of it, so the void does not discriminate between frameworks.
- **Capacity**: voids 122 of 125 by `count_section_files('voids')` at 10:47Z.
- **Verification**: 82 strings script-checked against saved raw text, all passed; 8 works read in full text, 6 at abstract only; Freud, Proust and C. S. Lewis reached only through carriers and marked so.
